# Log archive — k8s-platform-engineering

Older entries of [log](log.md), moved verbatim on 2026-11-26. Same append-only rule and header format `## [YYYY-MM-DD] <op> | <subject>`. This file holds everything from the bootstrap through the end of Batch 1 (mechanics and accuracy pass, gap closures, publishing setup). Newer entries stay in [log](log.md).

## [2026-11-25] bootstrap | k8s-platform-engineering

Decisions made during the interview:

- **Domain:** Kubernetes platform engineering principles: core architecture, KRM and API machinery, controller and operator mechanics, abstractions; architecture beneath Flux and Crossplane.
- **Scenario template:** Research.
- **Expected volume:** Large (500 or more sources).
- **Source types:** markdown and URL.
- **Page taxonomy:** concepts, sources, entities, examples, comparisons, decisions, findings, outputs (outputs on).
- **Seeded facts:** none.
- **Seeded gaps:** three (lifecycle trace, Compositions vs kro, scale and sharding).

Closing summary:

- Folders created: `raw/`, `concepts/`, `sources/`, `entities/`, `examples/`, `comparisons/`, `decisions/`, `findings/`, `outputs/`, `workflows/`, `.sticki/`.
- Staleness threshold: 180 days.
- `tier_threshold_sources`: 300.
- Write-authority table emitted to `CLAUDE.md`.
- Purpose template applied: Research.

The tested 5,000-node envelope and Gardener's 500-node advice are recorded as a finding and not as a contradiction, since they answer different questions. The seeded scale gap received a "largely helpful" note and was not closed, and the other two seeded gaps were not touched because the dossier does not help them. No Config Connector, ACK, Cluster API, or Backstage material appeared in the dossier, so those entities were not extended. Drafted inline because no page-writer subagent is available in this session.

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Full lifecycle trace from Git commit to Ready status
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled at the level of an assembled trace, not a single-source trace. Part 5 sections 2 to 4 ([outputs/k8s-platform-rampup/part-5-platform-architecture-principles-and-operating-at-scale](outputs/k8s-platform-rampup/part-5-platform-architecture-principles-and-operating-at-scale.md)) now walk one merged commit end to end: the source controller fetches the repository on its interval (or immediately when a notification Receiver fires) and packages an artifact; the Kustomization builds and applies it with server-side apply under a Flux field manager, runs an apply dry run each interval to detect drift, and impersonates a tenant ServiceAccount; built-in controllers then carry the intent forward through owner references. For the Crossplane half, the trace continues through the composite, the composition-function pipeline, managed resources and the provider's Connect, Observe, Create, Update, Delete cycle, and returns upward through status conditions (Ready, Reconciling, Stalled, Synced) read together with observedGeneration. Supporting pages: [entities/flux](entities/flux.md), [concepts/server-side-apply-and-field-ownership](concepts/server-side-apply-and-field-ownership.md), [concepts/finalizers](concepts/finalizers.md), [concepts/conditions](concepts/conditions.md), [examples/gitops-reverts-a-hand-edited-deployment](examples/gitops-reverts-a-hand-edited-deployment.md). Not settled: no single source traces this flow, so the trace is the wiki's own composition of documented behaviors and says where documentation is silent; finalizer behavior is covered on the deletion path (Crossplane managed resources, Flux prune and deletion policy), not as a registration step on create; and the how-to-close item for a worked example under examples/ was met by Part 5 instead of a standalone page. Remaining questions are tracked in [#flux-source-controller-internals-and-artifact-serving-under-leader-election](open-knowledge-gaps.md#flux-source-controller-internals-and-artifact-serving-under-leader-election--status-open) and [#flux-kustomization-finalizer-name-and-behavior](open-knowledge-gaps-archive.md#flux-kustomization-finalizer-name-and-behavior--status-closed-2026-11-26).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Crossplane Compositions vs kro dynamic resource graphs
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled as a structured comparison with a named decision. [comparisons/crossplane-composition-functions-versus-kro-resource-graph-execution-models](comparisons/crossplane-composition-functions-versus-kro-resource-graph-execution-models.md) sets the two side by side: Crossplane splits schema (XRD) from implementation (Composition) and runs a pipeline of gRPC functions in any language, leaving ordering to the functions and eventual reconciliation, with readiness from steps such as function-auto-ready and errors surfacing mostly at reconcile time; kro puts schema and implementation in one ResourceGraphDefinition, evaluates CEL expressions, infers a dependency graph from field references, creates in topological order and deletes in reverse, gates dependents with readyWhen, and validates at creation time. [comparisons/where-the-expressiveness-bound-sits-in-crossplane-functions-and-kro-cel](comparisons/where-the-expressiveness-bound-sits-in-crossplane-functions-and-kro-cel.md) explains the underlying trade (Crossplane bounds the contract and frees the code; kro bounds the language and frees the runtime), [comparisons/crossplane-xrds-versus-kro-resourcegraphdefinitions-as-crd-generators](comparisons/crossplane-xrds-versus-kro-resourcegraphdefinitions-as-crd-generators.md) covers how each generates its API, and [decisions/choose-kro-crossplane-or-both-for-a-platform-abstraction-layer](decisions/choose-kro-crossplane-or-both-for-a-platform-abstraction-layer.md) records the choice, including the function-kro bridge ([entities/function-kro](entities/function-kro.md)) that lets the two combine. Part 4 ([outputs/k8s-platform-rampup/part-4-platform-abstractions](outputs/k8s-platform-rampup/part-4-platform-abstractions.md)) teaches it. Not settled: the comparison is the wiki's synthesis, because no source compares the two head to head; kro is pre-1.0 (v1alpha1, version 0.10.0 in the docs read) so its facts may change; and kro dependency and readiness mechanics, status schema, released-version facts and per-RGD scale remain open in [#kro-dependency-graph-and-readiness-mechanics](open-knowledge-gaps-archive.md#kro-dependency-graph-and-readiness-mechanics--status-closed-2026-11-26), [#kro-status-schema-and-crossplane-v2-status-propagation](open-knowledge-gaps.md#kro-status-schema-and-crossplane-v2-status-propagation--status-open), [#kro-graph-api-status-and-released-version-facts](open-knowledge-gaps-archive.md#kro-graph-api-status-and-released-version-facts--status-closed-2026-11-26) and [#scaling-limits-of-per-rgd-kro-controllers-and-named-production-kro-adopters](open-knowledge-gaps.md#scaling-limits-of-per-rgd-kro-controllers-and-named-production-kro-adopters--status-open).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Scale: informers, rate-limited workqueues, and sharding
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled on mechanics and mitigations, not on measured limits. Part 5 sections 7 to 9 ([outputs/k8s-platform-rampup/part-5-platform-architecture-principles-and-operating-at-scale](outputs/k8s-platform-rampup/part-5-platform-architecture-principles-and-operating-at-scale.md)) explain the four in-controller limits (the informer cache holds every watched object with no eviction; the workqueue never processes one key concurrently so throughput follows worker count and reconcile time; client-side QPS and burst throttle before requests leave the process; leader election means extra replicas add failover, not capacity), the default workqueue limiter (per-item exponential backoff plus a token bucket), the separation of client-side throttling from server-side API Priority and Fairness, and 410 Gone relist storms, with the etcd and CRD-count limits underneath ([findings/etcd-scale-limits-of-the-shared-store](findings/etcd-scale-limits-of-the-shared-store.md), [findings/informer-caches-hold-every-watched-object-and-memory-grows-with-cluster-size](findings/informer-caches-hold-every-watched-object-and-memory-grows-with-cluster-size.md), [findings/client-side-throttling-and-server-side-apf-are-separate-layers](findings/client-side-throttling-and-server-side-apf-are-separate-layers.md)). Mitigations are documented per tool: Flux concurrency tuning and label sharding ([examples/flux-sharding-splits-reconciliation-across-label-selected-controller-instances](examples/flux-sharding-splits-reconciliation-across-label-selected-controller-instances.md), [decisions/shard-flux-controllers-or-tune-intervals-and-concurrency-first](decisions/shard-flux-controllers-or-tune-intervals-and-concurrency-first.md)), Argo CD cluster sharding ([comparisons/flux-label-sharding-versus-argo-cd-cluster-sharding](comparisons/flux-label-sharding-versus-argo-cd-cluster-sharding.md)), Crossplane provider families and activation policies, and kro's shared rate-limited queue. Not settled: most figures were read through model-generated digests and need rechecking ([findings/the-scale-dossier-relies-on-model-digests-for-most-numbers-so-limits-need-rechecking](findings/the-scale-dossier-relies-on-model-digests-for-most-numbers-so-limits-need-rechecking.md), [#scale-numbers-from-model-digests-need-rechecking](open-knowledge-gaps-archive.md#scale-numbers-from-model-digests-need-rechecking--status-closed-2026-11-26)); there are no independent benchmarks ([#independent-scale-benchmarks-for-etcd-vcluster-and-kcp](open-knowledge-gaps.md#independent-scale-benchmarks-for-etcd-vcluster-and-kcp--status-open)), no production Flux sharding numbers ([#named-flux-sharding-adopters-and-api-priority-and-fairness-incidents](open-knowledge-gaps.md#named-flux-sharding-adopters-and-api-priority-and-fairness-incidents--status-open)), and Crossplane v2 scaling guidance is unconfirmed ([#current-crossplane-v2-scaling-guidance](open-knowledge-gaps.md#current-crossplane-v2-scaling-guidance--status-open)).

- resolution: Settled: v2 XRDs have scope Namespaced or Cluster and reject claims; LegacyCluster exists only on the v1 XRD API and keeps claims ([findings/the-crossplane-xrd-docs-list-legacycluster-beside-v2-scopes-although-it-exists-only-on-the-v1-xrd-api](findings/the-crossplane-xrd-docs-list-legacycluster-beside-v2-scopes-although-it-exists-only-on-the-v1-xrd-api.md), [comparisons/crossplane-v1-claims-versus-v2-namespaced-composite-resources](comparisons/crossplane-v1-claims-versus-v2-namespaced-composite-resources.md)). Not settled: no removal date for claims was found.

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | managementPolicies feature status and the ObserveOnly-to-policy-list mapping
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled: managementPolicies is beta in v2.5; the mapping from older advice is an Observe list for observe-only, an empty list that pauses reconciliation, and Orphan meaning no Delete ([findings/older-observeonly-and-deletionpolicy-advice-needs-translation-to-managementpolicies](findings/older-observeonly-and-deletionpolicy-advice-needs-translation-to-managementpolicies.md), [decisions/choose-management-policies-for-an-imported-resource](decisions/choose-management-policies-for-an-imported-resource.md)). ## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Flux pre-dry-run manager takeover flag and current SSA documentation
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled: the flag is --override-manager-before-dry-run, first shipped in kustomize-controller v1.10.0 and Flux v2.10.0; it transfers all fields of the named managers and is not reverted if the apply fails ([findings/the-flux-pre-dry-run-takeover-transfers-all-fields-of-the-named-managers-and-is-not-reverted-if-the-apply-fails](findings/the-flux-pre-dry-run-takeover-transfers-all-fields-of-the-named-managers-and-is-not-reverted-if-the-apply-fails.md), [decisions/keep-flux-pre-dry-run-takeover-in-a-separate-opt-in-list-instead-of-extending-override-manager](decisions/keep-flux-pre-dry-run-takeover-in-a-separate-opt-in-list-instead-of-extending-override-manager.md)). Not settled: helm-controller driftDetection and Helm 4 server-side apply were not researched in this pass.

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Argo CD sharding algorithm maturity in current releases
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled: round-robin and consistent-hashing are still labelled experimental (Alpha) at v3.6.3, and legacy sharding can distribute clusters unevenly ([findings/argo-cd-legacy-sharding-can-be-uneven-and-the-other-algorithms-were-labelled-experimental](findings/argo-cd-legacy-sharding-can-be-uneven-and-the-other-algorithms-were-labelled-experimental.md)). Not settled: the v3.6.3 high-availability page no longer marks legacy as the default ([findings/the-argo-cd-v3-6-3-high-availability-page-no-longer-marks-legacy-as-the-default-sharding-method](findings/the-argo-cd-v3-6-3-high-availability-page-no-longer-marks-legacy-as-the-default-sharding-method.md)), so which method is the default is unconfirmed.

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Mutating Admission Policy release status
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled: Mutating Admission Policy is GA and on by default since v1.36 per the CHANGELOG and feature-gate table ([comparisons/admission-mechanisms-webhooks-vap-map-and-crd-rules](comparisons/admission-mechanisms-webhooks-vap-map-and-crd-rules.md)). Not settled: the Kubernetes docs pages disagree with the code on the first-available version ([findings/kubernetes-docs-pages-disagree-with-the-code-on-mutating-admission-policy-and-storage-version-migration-stage](findings/kubernetes-docs-pages-disagree-with-the-code-on-mutating-admission-policy-and-storage-version-migration-stage.md)).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | StorageVersionMigration GA details in Kubernetes v1.37
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled: storagemigration.k8s.io/v1 is GA in v1.37 and is user-triggered, so a retired CRD version needs a user-created migration object ([findings/storage-version-migration-is-not-triggered-automatically-so-a-retired-crd-version-needs-a-user-created-object](findings/storage-version-migration-is-not-triggered-automatically-so-a-retired-crd-version-needs-a-user-created-object.md), [concepts/crd-versioning-and-storage-migration](concepts/crd-versioning-and-storage-migration.md)). Not settled: the exact progress-condition field layout was not read.

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Requeue true backoff behavior in current controller-runtime
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled: at controller-runtime v0.25.3 returning Requeue true is a rate-limited requeue with per-item exponential backoff, and only RequeueAfter forgets the failure history ([findings/a-requeue-true-result-backs-off-exponentially-because-only-requeueafter-forgets-the-failure-history](findings/a-requeue-true-result-backs-off-exponentially-because-only-requeueafter-forgets-the-failure-history.md), [decisions/choose-error-requeueafter-or-terminalerror-in-a-reconciler](decisions/choose-error-requeueafter-or-terminalerror-in-a-reconciler.md)). Not settled: the origin of the older claim of an immediate requeue (issue 808) was not located.

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Current Cluster API MachineHealthCheck behavior
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled at Cluster API v1.15.0-rc.0: the v1beta2 names, a 100 percent default that leaves the short-circuit guard off so remediation is unbounded until a threshold is set, and an external remediation reference; v1beta1 maxUnhealthy and unhealthyRange are deprecated ([findings/the-cluster-api-machine-health-check-short-circuit-is-off-by-default-so-remediation-is-unbounded-until-a-threshold-is-set](findings/the-cluster-api-machine-health-check-short-circuit-is-off-by-default-so-remediation-is-unbounded-until-a-threshold-is-set.md), [findings/cluster-api-v1beta1-maxunhealthy-and-unhealthyrange-are-deprecated-and-older-material-uses-the-old-names](findings/cluster-api-v1beta1-maxunhealthy-and-unhealthyrange-are-deprecated-and-older-material-uses-the-old-names.md)). Not settled: these were read from a release candidate and need a recheck at v1.15.0 final.

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | ClusterClass maturity and the managed topologies proposal
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled at Cluster API v1.15.0-rc.0: ClusterTopology is Beta and on by default, while maturity labels disagree across doc versions and the original proposal still says provisional ([findings/cluster-api-clusterclass-maturity-labels-disagree-across-doc-versions-and-the-original-proposal-still-says-provisional](findings/cluster-api-clusterclass-maturity-labels-disagree-across-doc-versions-and-the-original-proposal-still-says-provisional.md), [examples/cluster-api-v1-15-0-rc-0-promotes-clustertopology-to-beta-and-adds-cel-health-check-rules](examples/cluster-api-v1-15-0-rc-0-promotes-clustertopology-to-beta-and-adds-cel-health-check-rules.md)). Not settled: only rc.0 existed, so the label needs a recheck at v1.15.0 final.

- resolution: Settled: every quoted scale figure was rechecked against raw page text and matched, with three wording tightenings: about 4 MiB per CRD, the Argo CD processor defaults, and the Crossplane memory figures, which are design-doc targets, not measurements ([findings/the-scale-dossier-relies-on-model-digests-for-most-numbers-so-limits-need-rechecking](findings/the-scale-dossier-relies-on-model-digests-for-most-numbers-so-limits-need-rechecking.md), [findings/crossplane-provider-memory-figures-are-design-doc-targets-not-measurements](findings/crossplane-provider-memory-figures-are-design-doc-targets-not-measurements.md)). ## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Primary incident reports for Skyscanner, Reddit, Jetstack, and Spotify
- closed by: Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled: all four primary reports are now read. Jetstack (2019 GKE master upgrade stalled on an unresponsive OPA validating webhook) was read earlier from a Wayback copy of the Venafi-hosted post. Skyscanner (2021 global outage: a template change dropped the {{ }} markers and Argo CD reconciled the result into deleting 478 services in every region, with service restored from a single region by about 2030 GMT) was read from a Wayback capture of the Medium post. Reddit (the 314-minute Pi-Day outage: a Kubernetes 1.24 upgrade removed the master node label that Calico route reflectors selected, and the lack of a supported downgrade forced a backup restore) was read from a Wayback capture of the r/RedditEng post. Spotify (a 50-node production cluster deleted from the wrong browser tab in November 2018, then two more clusters deleted through Terraform about a month later, with no end-user impact) was read from a user-supplied transcript and the speaker's slide deck. Pages: [examples/jetstack-s-2019-gke-master-upgrade-stalled-on-an-unresponsive-opa-validating-webhook](examples/jetstack-s-2019-gke-master-upgrade-stalled-on-an-unresponsive-opa-validating-webhook.md), [examples/skyscanner-s-2021-global-outage-an-incorrect-template-change-was-reconciled-by-argo-cd-into-deleting-478-services](examples/skyscanner-s-2021-global-outage-an-incorrect-template-change-was-reconciled-by-argo-cd-into-deleting-478-services.md), [examples/reddit-s-march-2023-pi-day-outage-a-kubernetes-1-24-upgrade-removed-the-master-node-label-that-calico-route-reflectors-selected](examples/reddit-s-march-2023-pi-day-outage-a-kubernetes-1-24-upgrade-removed-the-master-node-label-that-calico-route-reflectors-selected.md), [examples/spotify-deleted-one-production-gke-cluster-by-console-click-and-two-more-through-terraform-with-no-end-user-impact](examples/spotify-deleted-one-production-gke-cluster-by-console-click-and-two-more-through-terraform-with-no-end-user-impact.md). Not settled: the Spotify second incident is dated only as a month after November 2018; Reddit's year comes from the k8s.af index and the post's age, not the post text; and the Skyscanner post does not say which Argo CD sync settings were in use.

- rationale: A source-level read of seven ownership gaps. It shows kro sets no owner references on instance children and uses labels, an ApplySet inventory, and reverse waves, that the OwnerReferencesPermissionEnforcement plugin rejects a blockOwnerDeletion write without `update` on the owner's finalizers subresource and is off by default upstream, and that Argo CD prunes with foreground propagation by default and tracks by annotation. No dedicated KEP was found, and Crossplane namespace deletion with the provider absent stays undocumented. Two decisions list options with no recommendation. Two outputs todos name the part-3 and part-4 passages to revise, and no page in outputs/ was edited. Five draft pages were promoted to active.

- fact conflicts: 0
- gaps drafted: 0 (possible-resolution notes added under all five open gaps: SSA behavior in kro, ACK, Config Connector, and Helm 4; Cluster API field ownership and Argo CD Server-Side Diff stability; helm-controller override-manager and dry-run recovery; best default conflict policy; force versus strip-or-adopt; none was closed, and no closed gap was involved)

- rationale: A source-level read of server-side apply behavior across kro, ACK, Config Connector, Helm 4, helm-controller, Cluster API, and Argo CD. It settles each tool's manager and force behavior, shows that helm-controller's override flag acts before its dry-run diff, confirms Server-Side Diff as documented-stable with many open bugs, and records the two policy questions as evidence-only with no recommendation. Six draft pages were promoted to active. Unread items stay open: the Config Connector public docs, the per-service ACK controllers, the Helm v4.0.0 tag, a Cluster API field ownership table, and any named adopter policy.

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Argo CD finalizer variants and non-cascading deletes
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled. Argo CD has three cascade finalizer names, the bare `resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io` (documented default, foreground), `.../foreground`, and `.../background`, and the controller uses background propagation only for the exact `/background` name. Separate `pre-delete-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io` and `post-delete-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io` track hooks. Options for deleting an Application without its workloads are `argocd app delete APPNAME --cascade=false`, unsetting the finalizer before a kubectl delete, the UI choice Non-Cascading (Orphan), and `preserveResourcesOnDeletion` for ApplicationSets. The `PrunePropagationPolicy` sync option governs pruning during sync, not Application deletion. Not settled: whether the `/foreground` spelling differs from the bare name anywhere in the code, since no distinct branch was found. All reads were of the master branch on 2026-11-26. See [findings/argo-cd-applications-use-three-cascade-finalizer-names-and-a-non-cascading-delete-removes-the-finalizer](findings/argo-cd-applications-use-three-cascade-finalizer-names-and-a-non-cascading-delete-removes-the-finalizer.md) and [examples/argo-cd-resources-finalizer-cascades-an-application-delete-to-its-workloads](examples/argo-cd-resources-finalizer-cascades-an-application-delete-to-its-workloads.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Flux Kustomization finalizer name and behavior
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled. The Kustomization finalizer is `finalizers.fluxcd.io`, which the reconciler adds first to avoid a race with deletion. A suspended Kustomization or one with an empty inventory deletes nothing, MirrorPrune follows `spec.prune`, Delete and WaitForTermination always delete, and Orphan never does. WaitForTermination polls every 2 seconds up to the Kustomization timeout, and if the wait fails Flux logs an error and removes the finalizer anyway, so a timeout does not hold the Kustomization. A missing impersonation account also logs the stale objects and continues. Flux deletes by inventory and not owner references in the code read. Not settled: older beta API versions were not read. See [findings/flux-kustomization-uses-finalizers-fluxcd-io-and-removes-it-even-when-waitfortermination-times-out](findings/flux-kustomization-uses-finalizers-fluxcd-io-and-removes-it-even-when-waitfortermination-times-out.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Cluster API issue 1514 on blocking deletion with a finalizer or annotation
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled. Issue 1514 was opened 2019-10-10 and closed 2020-04-27 with a maintainer deferring to a CAEP. The thread weighed a named finalizer, a `Dependencies` list in the spec, a hook-registration CRD, and annotations. The implemented Machine Deletion Phase Hooks proposal chose annotations and rejects finalizers because "A misbehaving controller that accidentally removes finalizers could have undesirable effects." The current API defines the prefixes `pre-drain.delete.hook.machine.cluster.x-k8s.io` and `pre-terminate.delete.hook.machine.cluster.x-k8s.io`, and the control plane provider adds its own pre-terminate hook. Not settled: the Cluster API book page on the hooks could not be read, and the issue thread does not itself record the final choice. See [examples/cluster-api-issue-1514-was-closed-in-april-2020-in-favor-of-a-proposal-that-chose-annotation-hooks](examples/cluster-api-issue-1514-was-closed-in-april-2020-in-favor-of-a-proposal-that-chose-annotation-hooks.md) and [findings/cluster-api-chose-annotation-hooks-over-finalizers-for-machine-deletion-because-a-misbehaving-controller-could-remove-finalizers](findings/cluster-api-chose-annotation-hooks-over-finalizers-for-machine-deletion-because-a-misbehaving-controller-could-remove-finalizers.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | How kro sets ownership and labels on child resources
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Largely settled. kro v0.10.0 documents that it does not set owner references on instance children, for ordered deletion and because a namespaced owner cannot own a cluster-scoped child. It stamps `kro.run/owned`, instance identity labels, `app.kubernetes.io/managed-by: kro`, and `applyset.kubernetes.io/part-of`, stores each child's wave in `internal.kro.run/apply-order`, and deletes in reverse waves from the persisted ApplySet inventory. It does set an RGD owner reference on the generated CRD and GraphRevisions, and the FAQ shows manual owner references and a tracking-id annotation for Argo CD. Not settled: KREP-004 and KREP-014 were not read. See [findings/kro-sets-no-owner-references-on-instance-children-and-tracks-them-with-labels-and-an-applyset-inventory](findings/kro-sets-no-owner-references-on-instance-children-and-tracks-them-with-labels-and-an-applyset-inventory.md) and [comparisons/how-tools-garbage-collect-owner-references-inventory-and-finalizers](comparisons/how-tools-garbage-collect-owner-references-inventory-and-finalizers.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Argo CD PrunePropagationPolicy and resource tracking methods
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Largely settled. Prune uses foreground deletion by default, and `PrunePropagationPolicy` accepts background, foreground, and orphan, with foreground as the fallback in source. Tracking is `annotation` (default), `annotation+label` (label informational, truncated at 63 characters), or `label`, set by `application.resourceTrackingMethod`, and a tracking annotation that does not reference its own resource keeps the resource out of sync status and pruning. Not settled: the docs were read from `master` (latest release v3.6.3), and nothing states how tracking methods interact with owner-reference-based child discovery. See [findings/argo-cd-tracks-resources-by-annotation-by-default-and-label-mode-truncates-and-collides-with-other-tools](findings/argo-cd-tracks-resources-by-annotation-by-default-and-label-mode-truncates-and-collides-with-other-tools.md) and [decisions/choose-an-argo-cd-resource-tracking-method-annotation-annotation-plus-label-or-label](decisions/choose-an-argo-cd-resource-tracking-method-annotation-annotation-plus-label-or-label.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | kstatus rules for combining observedGeneration and conditions
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled from source. `status.Compute` in cli-utils checks the deletion timestamp (Terminating), then compares `metadata.generation` with `status.observedGeneration` (a mismatch gives InProgress with reason `LatestGenerationNotObserved`, and the check is skipped when the status field is absent), then scans conditions in array order (`Reconciling=True` gives InProgress and `Stalled=True` gives Failed, first match wins), then applies built-in rules, then a `Ready` fallback (True gives Current, False and Unknown give InProgress, never Failed), and otherwise reports Current. A condition's own `observedGeneration` is never read because kstatus's condition struct has no such field. That a stale top-level generation overrides `Ready=True` is an inference from code order. Not settled: no incident shows the premature-readiness path on a named tool beyond the issues already recorded. See [entities/kstatus](entities/kstatus.md), [findings/kstatus-checks-deletion-then-generation-then-reconciling-and-stalled-then-ready-so-a-stale-top-level-observedgeneration-overrides-ready-true](findings/kstatus-checks-deletion-then-generation-then-reconciling-and-stalled-then-ready-so-a-stale-top-level-observedgeneration-overrides-ready-true.md), and [findings/a-kstatus-resource-whose-controller-never-writes-status-observedgeneration-skips-the-generation-check-and-can-read-current-early](findings/a-kstatus-resource-whose-controller-never-writes-status-observedgeneration-skips-the-generation-check-and-can-read-current-early.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Which parts of the Config Connector reconcile-status design shipped
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled for direct controllers at release v1.162.0. Shipped as designed: one `Ready` condition at a time, `Updating` and `UpToDate` reasons, a top-level `status.observedGeneration` on every status update, and events with the same reasons. Shipped differently: the failure reason is `UpdateFailed` (not `UpdateFailure`), and the messages are "Update call failed: ...", "Update in progress", and "The resource is up to date". Not found in code: the first-field-path message, a field-path helper, the `Updating:<operation-id>` reason (a future consideration in the design itself), and a per-condition `observedGeneration`. Caveat: the searches used GitHub code search, which may lag, and whether an open pull request implements the design was not checked. The question's wording `UpdateFailure` is the design's name. See [findings/config-connector-shipped-updatefailed-with-a-single-ready-condition-and-the-first-field-path-message-and-operation-id-reason-are-not-in-code](findings/config-connector-shipped-updatefailed-with-a-single-ready-condition-and-the-first-field-path-message-and-operation-id-reason-are-not-in-code.md) and [examples/config-connector-ready-condition-moves-through-updating-uptodate-and-updatefailure](examples/config-connector-ready-condition-moves-through-updating-uptodate-and-updatefailure.md) (now marked contradicted).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Informer resync semantics
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled from client-go and controller-runtime source. A resync delivers an update notification for every object in the informer's local cache and adds no interaction with the authoritative storage, `DeltaFIFO.Resync` re-queues keys from the local known-objects store, a zero period means no resync, and `HasSynced` is unrelated to resync. controller-runtime's `SyncPeriod` defaults to 10 hours with jitter, emits an artificial Update event that `GenerationChangedPredicate` filters out, and the documentation recommends `RequeueAfter` over shortening it. The word names different mechanisms in other tools: ACK's resync is a `RequeueAfter` and kro's flag text says "re list". Not settled: the source was read on default branches and not pinned to release tags. See [findings/a-resync-replays-the-informers-local-cache-to-the-handlers-and-never-contacts-the-api-server](findings/a-resync-replays-the-informers-local-cache-to-the-handlers-and-never-contacts-the-api-server.md), [findings/the-word-resync-names-three-different-mechanisms-in-client-go-kro-and-ack](findings/the-word-resync-names-three-different-mechanisms-in-client-go-kro-and-ack.md), and [decisions/keep-informer-resync-disabled-or-set-a-periodic-resync](decisions/keep-informer-resync-disabled-or-set-a-periodic-resync.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Informer and cache choices of kro, Config Connector, ACK, and Cluster API
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled from source for the four tools. kro holds one metadata-only informer per GVR, Config Connector caches only its own configuration kinds, ACK scopes by namespace and label selector, and Cluster API caches only cluster-labelled Secrets and gives each workload cluster its own cache, with rate limits and concurrency defaults in the comparison table. Not settled: no production memory figures were found, and Backstage was not researched. See [comparisons/informer-and-cache-choices-across-kro-config-connector-ack-and-cluster-api](comparisons/informer-and-cache-choices-across-kro-config-connector-ack-and-cluster-api.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | controller-runtime v0.3.0 dynamic mapper claim and aggregated discovery rationale
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled except for one detail. The v0.3.0 release notes (2019-10-10) list the `DynamicRESTMapper` (#554), and the v0.3.0 tag's `manager.go` returns it as the default. KEP-3352 gives the aggregated discovery rationale, a storm of requests with latency and throttling, with alpha v1.26, beta v1.27 and stable v1.30. Not settled: which controller-runtime release adopted aggregated discovery was not established, and the KEP does not mention controller-runtime's mapper. See [findings/the-dynamic-restmapper-is-confirmed-as-the-controller-runtime-default-at-v0-3-0-from-the-release-notes-and-tag-source](findings/the-dynamic-restmapper-is-confirmed-as-the-controller-runtime-default-at-v0-3-0-from-the-release-notes-and-tag-source.md) and [findings/aggregated-discovery-exists-because-per-group-version-discovery-caused-a-request-storm](findings/aggregated-discovery-exists-because-per-group-version-discovery-caused-a-request-storm.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Admission behavior of Crossplane, kro, Argo CD, Config Connector, ACK, Cluster API, and Gatekeeper
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Largely settled. Crossplane ships one Fail Usage webhook and uses CEL for XRD and Composition validation. kro and ACK ship no webhook in the files read, and kro relies on CRD schema and CEL. Config Connector and Cluster API ship many webhooks, all Fail. Gatekeeper defaults to Ignore with a documented fail-closed path. Not settled: Argo CD webhook absence is UNVERIFIED because only tree paths were searched, and namespace exclusions in the Cluster API and Config Connector manifests were not searched exhaustively. See [comparisons/admission-webhooks-and-failure-policies-across-crossplane-kro-argo-cd-config-connector-ack-cluster-api-gatekeeper-and-kyverno](comparisons/admission-webhooks-and-failure-policies-across-crossplane-kro-argo-cd-config-connector-ack-cluster-api-gatekeeper-and-kyverno.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | CEL cost limits and per-request budgets
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled from Kubernetes source. The static estimate limit is 10,000,000 per expression and 100,000,000 per CRD, the runtime limit is 1,000,000 per call, the runtime budget is 10,000,000 per binding or custom resource, and matchConditions have 2,500,000. The CRD docs advise `maxItems`, `maxProperties` and `maxLength` because unbounded inputs get worst-case cost. Values were read on master, not pinned to a release tag. See [findings/cel-cost-limits-are-10-million-per-expression-and-100-million-per-crd-statically-and-1-million-per-call-and-10-million-per-request-at-runtime](findings/cel-cost-limits-are-10-million-per-expression-and-100-million-per-crd-statically-and-1-million-per-call-and-10-million-per-request-at-runtime.md) and [findings/an-unbounded-list-or-string-makes-the-cel-cost-estimate-reject-a-crd-rule-until-maxitems-and-maxlength-are-set](findings/an-unbounded-list-or-string-makes-the-cel-cost-estimate-reject-a-crd-rule-until-maxitems-and-maxlength-are-set.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Status of the conversion webhook and garbage collection problem
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Mostly settled. The stall is fixed upstream: pull request 125796 merged on 2024-09-25 with milestone v1.32, issue 101078 closed as completed, and the 1.32 changelog carries the release note. The caveat is that objects in the unsynced resource type with owner references that set `blockOwnerDeletion: true` are not known to the garbage collector. Not settled: how long dependents remain after a down webhook, and any further partial-informer work. See [findings/since-kubernetes-1-32-the-garbage-collector-proceeds-after-a-failed-cache-sync-but-cannot-see-block-owner-deletion-owners-of-the-unsynced-type](findings/since-kubernetes-1-32-the-garbage-collector-proceeds-after-a-failed-cache-sync-but-cannot-see-block-owner-deletion-owners-of-the-unsynced-type.md).

## [2026-11-26] gaps | close | Config Connector conflict handling when two objects target one cloud resource
- closed by Eric Shamay
- resolution: Settled from documentation. Config Connector documents a namespace-scoped lease in the labels `cnrm-lease-holder-id` and `cnrm-lease-expiration` (40 minutes), the status `ManagementConflict`, and the annotation `cnrm.cloud.google.com/management-conflict-prevention-policy` with the values `resource` or `none` and a default of `none`. Prevention does not work for resources without label support, and same-namespace `resourceID` conflicts cannot be managed. The behavior was read, not tested, and the same-namespace different-name case is undocumented. See [findings/config-connector-conflict-prevention-defaults-to-none-so-two-objects-can-manage-one-cloud-resource-unless-a-lease-policy-is-set](findings/config-connector-conflict-prevention-defaults-to-none-so-two-objects-can-manage-one-cloud-resource-unless-a-lease-policy-is-set.md) and [decisions/set-config-connector-management-conflict-prevention-to-none-or-resource-or-avoid-acquisition](decisions/set-config-connector-management-conflict-prevention-to-none-or-resource-or-avoid-acquisition.md).
